[48;2;55;55;55m                                                                                                    [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153mYou are a research librarian and a Hebrew Bible scholar, with working knowledge of Semitic [39m       [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153mphilology, cognitive linguistics, and the philosophy of emotion. Your job in this session is [39m     [49m

[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153mliterature**, and to report that honestly.[39m                                                        [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m                                                                                                    [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153m## Why this matters[39m                                                                               [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m                                                                                                    [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153mThe author of the idea is an independent researcher who is considering presenting it to academic [39m [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153mlinguists and Hebrew Bible scholars. Before doing so, the author needs to know whether the idea [39m  [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153mis new, partly anticipated, or already published. An inflated "nobody has said this" is far more [39m [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153mharmful to the author than an honest "X said something close in 1987". **Finding prior art is a [39m  [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153msuccess, not a failure.** Missing it would embarrass the author in front of the very scholars [39m    [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153mthey want to approach.[39m                                                                            [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m                                                                                                    [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153m## The idea being checked[39m                                                                         [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m                                                                                                    [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153m> **Nefesh is the lived experience of a living creature's striving to persist in its life (the [39m   [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153mdrive to self-preservation).**[39m                                                                    [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153m> It is not an entity the creature *has*; it is the creature itself, viewed as one who needs, [39m    [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153mdesires, is threatened, and recovers. The throat, mouth and breath are where this striving is [39m    [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153mfelt; blood is the substance it depends on.[39m                                                       [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m                                                                                                    [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153mThe idea claims that this single schema motivates the whole semantic range of *nefesh*:[39m           [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m                                                                                                    [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153m1. **Breath**: it goes in, stops, and leaves at death (Gen 35:18; Ps 69:2).[39m                       [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153m2. **Appetite**: the drive to swallow, eat and drink, extended metaphorically (Prov 27:7; Isa [39m    [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153m5:14, Sheol's *nefesh*).[39m                                                                          [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153m3. **Restoration**: the Niphal verb *wayyinnāpēš*, "catch one's breath / recover", in Exod 23:12,[39m [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153mExod 31:17 (said of God) and 2 Sam 16:14. This is read as a return to equilibrium, i.e. [39m          [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153mhomeostasis.[39m                                                                                      [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153m4. **Attraction and aversion**: disgust and loathing verbs (*gāʿal*, *qûṣ*, *qāṣar*). This is the[39m [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153m*only* field applied to God's *nefesh*, and is read as a protective response.[39m                     [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153m5. **Life itself**: sought, rescued, ransomed; *nefesh* is named exactly when life is at stake [39m   [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153m(Gen 19:17; Exod 21:23).[39m                                                                          [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153m6. **Livelihood**: Deut 24:6, the millstone "is *nefesh*". Compare Akkadian *napištu*, [39m           [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153m"livelihood, provisions".[39m                                                                         [49m
[48;2;55;55;55m  [38;2;153;153;153m7. **Blood**: shedding it harms the striving (Lev 17:11; Deut 12:23).[39m                             [49m
